## Consequential corrections

- Scan 079, local_label: `\unsure{\kappa&#124;k}_{i_0}`. The label has a straight upright with two right branches; the k inside T_infinity(k), in the same crop, has a conspicuous looped ascender. This favors kappa but is insufficient to exclude an unlooped Latin k. The first reading is retained with explicit doubt.
- Scan 079, local_target: `\pi_1(U_{\unsure{\eta&#124;k}})`. Blind review: the very small subscript has a short rising stroke and a forked/hooked right leg; its resolution does not establish the long eta descender securely. The neighbouring full-size k is looped, but this does not exclude a compressed k. Eta remains the preferred reading, with k retained explicitly.
- Scan 079, vertical_equality: `T \arrow[d,equal] T_\infty(k)`. T_infinity(k) is on the row below T, with two short upright parallel strokes between the rows. There are no enclosing parentheses around the identity and no horizontal equals sign. The right arrow from T to pi_1(U_eta) has two distinct diagonal barbs.
- Scan 079, deleted_margin_state: `\deleted{* En fait, on n'a pas ... \unsure{comme}}`. A long boundary stroke encloses the six-line note and several long oblique cancellation strokes traverse this entire block, including its opening area. The initial star and En fait belong to the cancelled block, not the later unstruck note beginning en tous cas.
- Scan 079, deleted_margin_words: `pour les situations fibrées, on trouve une ext. du pi_1 de la base`. After pour, the small tall l and following low es give les. Situations has the terminal s and fibrées ends in a low s before the comma. On the next line the initial oval and low n give on, followed by tr- and the connected ouve ending of trouve; the word before pi_1 has an ascender d followed by u, not es. The cross-out passes through portions but these opposed readings are recoverable. The final comme remains doubtful.

On the rotated marginal crop, the initial i/l/y/a are separate forms, then the crossed t and descending j of tjrs and the low humps of une. The next word shows the arithmetisation letter chain, including the tall th and t strokes. There is no detached accent over the first e and no opening quote pair before its a; two closing ticks after the word are plain. Neither si!? nor très nor réduits matches these strokes.
- Scan 101, action_effective: `action \inserted{effective}`. Action is on the body line. Above its right end, the inserted word has two long f stems, low e/c, crossed t, i dot and v/e ending. Its curved leader descends between action and de. The placement and final v/e strokes exclude an inserted affection.
- Scan 101, colon_after_structure: `:`. After structure, two compact detached marks are vertically aligned. The lower mark has no loop or body of a. The nearby à before tâter has an independently visible small a body beneath its accent, providing a same-page contrast. The colon is retained despite the unusual syntax.
- Scan 101, conjecture_heading: `\uline{Conjecture-à-tout-hasard}`. After Conjecture, the crop shows a short dash, accented à, another dash, two t uprights around the low ou of tout, another dash, and hasard with its tall h and final d. One continuous underline lies beneath the title and stops before its colon. H’s de bonne réduction is not written.
- Scan 102, opening_tt: `tt`. Two upright stems have separate hooked feet and one shared crossbar. Their lower ends do not join into the V-shaped apex required by a universal quantifier. The paired t structure can also be compared locally with quitte in the e_quitte crop; no extra e follows the abbreviation.
- Scan 102, opening_inserted: `\inserted{(ou un)}`. The raised line has its own opening and closing parentheses; within them are a small o/u group and a separate two-hump un. No tall l supports seul. A separate long curved leader below the raised words returns to the gap after tt and before scindage; that leader is not an extra textual parenthesis.
- Scan 102, opening_U_eta: `U_\eta`. After U the low index has a single hump and a long descending right leg curling left. No independent upper bar is present. The clear barred eta forms in c) and d) provide direct local comparisons for both the letter and the missing bar here.
- Scan 102, c_bar_eta: `\pi_{\mathrm{ab}}=H_1(U_{\bar\eta})`. The subscript after U has one low n-like hump and a long curved descending right leg, with a separate horizontal bar above it. There are no stacked turns of xi. The small 1 after H and the ab after pi are also visible.
- Scan 102, d_bar_eta: `H_{\unsure{1&#124;n}}(U_{\bar\eta},T_\ell)`. The H subscript has a diagonal rising entry, a short downstroke and a low right foot. This can be a flourished1, but it also presents the joined two-stroke outline of n; it is less plain than the isolated1 in c). Retain1 first with n explicit. The remaining formula has the separate bar and descending eta, comma, and a tall T with one crossbar and a low looped ell. Those surrounding signs are unchanged.
- Scan 102, d_cancelled: `\deleted{\unsure{Pour car. $p>0$&#124;Pour $\ell\ne p$&#124;Pour $\ell=p$}}`. The long cancellation crosses the low baseline after underlined NB. An initial looped P and the o/u/r run remain, then a low open c-like entry, merged short strokes and a long p-like descender favor Pour car. p>0. The crossed low group could instead be a collapsed ell followed by an obscured relation and p, so retain the blind ell-ne-p and ell=p alternatives inside the same deletion. None of these cancelled readings is secure letter by letter. The raised ell=p>0 is clear and live. The following detached descending curve is shorter and lower than the full opening parenthesis, favoring a comma, but its sweep also admits a closing parenthesis; retain that punctuation alternative without balancing the clause.

Its curved leader lands after ouvert and before au besoin. Before that leader, after the terminal t of ouvert, no separate comma is visible; the only comma belonging to this join is the one written after the inserted E_k.

The long slanting leader returns to the gap after baseline sur and before baseline une; une itself remains on the lower writing line, followed by extension finie de k. There is no extra baseline une before the raised k.
- Scan 102, f_negative_condition: `\inserted{n'}est \inserted{pas trop} loufoque`. The baseline has the low e/s and tall crossed t of est, then loufoque with its looped l, tall f descender and separate q descender. A raised n with apostrophe has a V-shaped leader into the gap before est. The separate raised pas trop has p descenders and crossed t; its long curved leader lands between est and loufoque. Neither insertion is cancelled.
- Scan 102, f_caution: `à définir convenablement`. After a single opening parenthesis is a small a with detached accent, then définir with a d loop, f descender, dotted i and short final r. The visible initial segment convenab at the right continues on the short lower baseline as lement; no written hyphen is supplied. One exclamation and the closing parenthesis complete the remark. There is no extra remarque or second opening parenthesis.
- Scan 102, d_where_or_if: `si`. After the separate comma, the initial letter is a short open bent stroke rather than a closed o. The following flattened i has a distinct dense dot directly above it, not a grave stroke over a u. Resolve the supplied où|si mark to si, following the blind reading.

### Changes after the blind second reading

| Box | Frozen first reading | Blind second reading | Final reading | Decision and crop evidence |

| B002 | `&#96;&#96;admissible'' \unsure{?}, i e.` | `&#96;&#96;admissible'' \unsure{?&#124;\deleted{?}}, i e.` | `&#96;&#96;admissible'' \unsure{?&#124;\deleted{?}}, i e.` | Two short quote strokes stand before and after admissible. The following question-shaped mark has a dot and several overlapping loops/strokes through its body; retracing and cancellation cannot be distinguished. Retain the question sign first and explicitly admit its cancelled state, as the blind reader proposed. A separate comma follows. In i e., the first point is above the i rather than on the baseline; only the point after e is an abbreviation stop. [crops/second_149_scan101_B002.png] |
| B004 | `si ce n’est la courbe` | `si on vise la courbe` | `si on vise la courbe` | The first group is si with its detached i dot. Next is a low rounded entry and n return, on. The following group has a shallow v, a dotted i and the s/e return; it lacks the tall terminal crossed t required by n-est. Read si on vise, following the independent reader, rather than si ce n-est. La courbe follows on the same baseline. [crops/second_149_scan101_B004.png] |
| B012 | `\unsure{Sans&#124;\deleted{Sans}} m’y attendre, me voici à tâter un peu la structure : l’$\infty$ des variétés` | `\unsure{Sans&#124;\deleted{Sans}} m'y attendre, me voici à tâter un<br>peu la structure : l'os des \unsure{variétés&#124;varietés}` | `\unsure{Sans&#124;\deleted{Sans}} m’y attendre, me voici à tâter un peu la structure : l’\unsure{$\infty$&#124;os} des variétés` | The passage has me voici with its visible dots, a circumflex over tater and a colon after structure; the e of varietes has a separate accent stroke. The tiny form after l-apostrophe has a closed left loop, a diagonal join/crossing and a lower right bowl whose upper side is open. This favors the first infinity-symbol reading but can also be the joined letters os. The focused root crop crops/audit_149_scan101_infinity_word.png at original pixels(1025,1340,1275,1470) confirms the open right bowl and cannot fully separate those possibilities. Retain infinity first with os explicit, without selecting by the expected meaning of the sentence. The surrounding words and Sans-state uncertainty remain. [crops/second_149_scan101_B012.png] |
| B013 | `Teichm\unsure{ü&#124;ù}ller !` | `Teichm\unsure{ù&#124;ü}ller !` | `Teichm\unsure{ù&#124;ü}ller !` | The name has the same clear T/e/i/c/h/m/u/l/l/e/r sequence in both readings. Do not supply a standard spelling from the proper name. The terminal exclamation is unchanged. [crops/second_149_scan101_B013.png] |
